redirectPath = '/no-access';\n1688\t        }\n1689\t        break;\n1690\t      default:\n1691\t        redirectPath = '/';\n1692\t    }\n1693\t\n1694\t    return {\n1695\t      user,\n1696\t where: { id: userId },\n1704\t    });\n1705\t  }\n1706\t\n1707\t  /**\n1708\t   * Handle failed login with atomic database increment to prevent race conditions.\n1709\t   * Uses a single atomic SQL statement with RETURNING to avoid multiple round-trips (MED-01).\n1710\t   * Returns the updated failedLoginAttempts count for accurate audit logging (M-04).\n1711\t   */\n1712\t  private async handleFailedLogin(user: User): Promise<number> {\n1713\t    const lockoutUntil = new Date(Date.now() + this.lockoutDurationMinutes * 60 * 1000);\n1714\t\n1715\t    // Single atomic query: increment + conditional lockout + return updated values (MED-01)\n1716\t    //\n1717\t    // ORPHAN-HIGH-318 — WHY updateReturningRows instead of a hand-written\n1718\t    // type annotation: the postgres driver returns `[rows, affectedCount]`\n1719\t    // for UPDATE statements, NOT the rows array. The previous\n1720\t    // `Array<{...}>` annotation asserted the wrong shape, so `result[0]`\n1721\t    // was the rows ARRAY: every audit event recorded \"attempt 0\" and the\n1722\t    // CRITICAL ACCOUNT_LOCKED emission below never fired in production\n1723\t    // (0 >= maxFailedAttempts is always false). The helper runtime-asserts\n1724\t    // the tuple shape before any field access.\n1725\t    const raw: unknown = await this.dataSource.query(\n1726\t      // SEC-LOW-001(c) — WHY $3::timestamptz (not ::timestamp): users.lockedUntil\n1727\t      // is a TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE column and $3 is bound to a JS Date (an\n1728\t      // absolute instant). Casting to ::timestamp (without tz) drops the offset\n1729\t      // and reinterprets the lockout deadline under the DB session TimeZone,\n1730\t      // drifting the lockout window on any non-UTC session. ::timestamptz\n1731\t      // round-trips the instant losslessly.\n1732\t      `UPDATE auth.users\n1733\t       SET \"failedLoginAttempts\" = \"failedLoginAttempts\" + 1,\n1734\t           \"lockedUntil\" = CASE\n1735\t             WHEN \"failedLoginAttempts\" + 1 >= $2 THEN $3::timestamptz\n1736\t             ELSE \"lockedUntil\"\n1737\t           END\n1738\t       WHERE id = $1\n1739\t       RETURNING \"failedLoginAttempts\", \"lockedUntil\"`,\n1740\t      [user.id, this.maxFailedAttempts, lockoutUntil],\n1741\t    );\n1742\t    // lockedUntil arrives as Date (pg parses timestamptz) — typed as\n1743\t    // Date | string | null defensively is WRONG per repo rules; the pg\n1744\t    // driver contract is Date | null for timestamptz. `!= null` (not\n1745\t    // `!== null`) also rejects undefined, which would indicate a\n1746\t    // RETURNING-clause drift rather than an unlocked account.\n1747\t    const result = updateReturningRows<{\n1748\t      failedLoginAttempts: number;\n1749\t      lockedUntil: Date | null;\n1750\t    }>(raw);\n1751\t\n1752\t    const updatedAttempts = result[0]?.failedLoginAttempts ?? 0;\n1753\t    const isNowLocked = result[0]?.lockedUntil != null && updatedAttempts >= this.maxFailedAttempts;\n1754\t\n1755\t    if (isNowLocked) {\n1756\t      // SECURITY: Log user ID instead of email to prevent PII exposure (H-14)\n1757\t      this.logger.warn(`Account locked for userId=${user.id} until ${lockoutUntil.toISOString()}`);\n1758\t      // SECURITY AUDIT: Log account lockout (critical security event)\n1759\t      await this.logSecurityEvent(\n1760\t        'ACCOUNT_LOCKED',\n1761\t        {\n1762\t          userId: user.id,\n1763\t          email: user.email,\n1764\t          tenantId: user.tenantId,\n1765\t          success: false,\n1766\t          reason: `Account locked after ${this.maxFailedAttempts} failed attempts. Locked until ${lockoutUntil.toISOString()}`,\n1767\t        },\n1768\t        AuditLogSeverity.CRITICAL,\n1769\t      );"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"66962382-f920-48a2-964d-9f75acea2f64","uuid":"7b78ce48-e81f-43db-ae26-405965ee22ce","timestamp":"2026-09-21T01:31:14.035Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-214613d2c9c1/apps/auth-service/src/modules/authentication/services/authentication.service.ts","content":"          event: 'token_validation_failed',\n          errorType: error instanceof Error ? error.name : 'UnknownError',\n        }),\n      );\n      return { valid: false };\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Get current user with their accessible modules\n   */\n  async me(userId: string, effectiveTenantId?: string | null): Promise<MePayload> {\n    const user = await this.userRepository.findOne({\n      where: { id: userId },\n    });\n\n    if (!user) {\n      // SECURITY: Generic message to prevent information leakage\n      throw new UnauthorizedException(GENERIC_AUTH_ERROR_MSG);\n    }\n\n    // The JWT tenant claim is the authoritative effective tenant for the session;\n    // `me` reports it (not only the DB record) so the frontend always scopes its\n    // queries to the token's tenant. For a regular user the token tenant equals\n    // the DB tenant (no-op); a platform SUPER_ADMIN has a null token tenant.\n    if (effectiveTenantId) {\n      user.tenantId = effectiveTenantId;\n    }\n\n    // Get user's accessible modules\n    const modules = await this.tokenService.getUserModules(user);\n\n    // Determine redirect path based on role\n    let redirectPath: string;\n    switch (user.role) {\n      case Role.SUPER_ADMIN:\n        redirectPath = '/admin/dashboard';\n        break;\n      case Role.TENANT_ADMIN:\n        redirectPath = '/tenant/dashboard';\n        break;\n      case Role.MODULE_MANAGER:\n      case Role.MODULE_USER:\n        // Redirect to first/primary module\n        if (modules.length > 0 && modules[0]) {\n          redirectPath = modules[0].defaultRoute;\n        } else {\n          redirectPath = '/no-access';\n        }\n        break;\n      default:\n        redirectPath = '/';\n    }\n\n    return {\n      user,\n      modules,\n      redirectPath,\n    };\n  }\n\n  async getUserById(userId: string): Promise<User | null> {\n    return this.userRepository.findOne({\n      where: { id: userId },\n    });\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Handle failed login with atomic database increment to prevent race conditions.\n   * Uses a single atomic SQL statement with RETURNING to avoid multiple round-trips (MED-01).\n   * Returns the updated failedLoginAttempts count for accurate audit logging (M-04).\n   */\n  private async handleFailedLogin(user: User): Promise<number> {\n    const lockoutUntil = new Date(Date.now() + this.lockoutDurationMinutes * 60 * 1000);\n\n    // Single atomic query: increment + conditional lockout + return updated values (MED-01)\n    //\n    // ORPHAN-HIGH-318 — WHY updateReturningRows instead of a hand-written\n    // type annotation: the postgres driver returns `[rows, affectedCount]`\n    // for UPDATE statements, NOT the rows array. The previous\n    // `Array<{...}>` annotation asserted the wrong shape, so `result[0]`\n    // was the rows ARRAY: every audit event recorded \"attempt 0\" and the\n    // CRITICAL ACCOUNT_LOCKED emission below never fired in production\n    // (0 >= maxFailedAttempts is always false). The helper runtime-asserts\n    // the tuple shape before any field access.\n    const raw: unknown = await this.dataSource.query(\n      // SEC-LOW-001(c) — WHY $3::timestamptz (not ::timestamp): users.lockedUntil\n      // is a TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE column and $3 is bound to a JS Date (an\n      // absolute instant). Casting to ::timestamp (without tz) drops the offset\n      // and reinterprets the lockout deadline under the DB session TimeZone,\n      // drifting the lockout window on any non-UTC session. ::timestamptz\n      // round-trips the instant losslessly.\n      `UPDATE auth.users\n       SET \"failedLoginAttempts\" = \"failedLoginAttempts\" + 1,\n           \"lockedUntil\" = CASE\n             WHEN \"failedLoginAttempts\" + 1 >= $2 THEN $3::timestamptz\n             ELSE \"lockedUntil\"\n           END\n       WHERE id = $1\n       RETURNING \"failedLoginAttempts\", \"lockedUntil\"`,\n      [user.id, this.maxFailedAttempts, lockoutUntil],\n    );\n    // lockedUntil arrives as Date (pg parses timestamptz) — typed as\n    // Date | string | null defensively is WRONG per repo rules; the pg\n    // driver contract is Date | null for timestamptz. `!= null` (not\n    // `!== null`) also rejects undefined, which would indicate a\n    // RETURNING-clause drift rather than an unlocked account.\n    const result = updateReturningRows<{\n      failedLoginAttempts: number;\n      lockedUntil: Date | null;\n    }>(raw);\n\n    const updatedAttempts = result[0]?.failedLoginAttempts ?? 0;\n    const isNowLocked = result[0]?.lockedUntil != null && updatedAttempts >= this.maxFailedAttempts;\n\n    if (isNowLocked) {\n      // SECURITY: Log user ID instead of email to prevent PII exposure (H-14)\n      this.logger.warn(`Account locked for userId=${user.id} until ${lockoutUntil.toISOString()}`);\n      // SECURITY AUDIT: Log account lockout (critical security event)\n      await this.logSecurityEvent(\n        'ACCOUNT_LOCKED',\n        {\n          userId: user.id,\n          email: user.email,\n          tenantId: user.tenantId,\n          success: false,\n          reason: `Account locked after ${this.maxFailedAttempts} failed attempts. Locked until ${lockoutUntil.toISOString()}`,\n        },\n        AuditLogSeverity.CRITICAL,\n      );","numLines":130,"startLine":1640,"totalLines":2189}}}

readonly readPaths: string[];\n}\n\nconst REPOSITORY_METHODS = new Set(['find', 'findOne', 'findOneBy', 'findBy', 'count', 'update', 'delete', 'softDelete']);\nconst RAW_QUERY_METHODS = new Set(['query']);\nconst TENANT_GUARD_NAMES = new Set(['TenantGuard', 'GqlTenantGuard']);\n\nexport function analyzeTenantScoping(input: AdapterInput, workspaceRoot = process.cwd()): AriaOutput {\n  const roots = requireScanRoots('tenant-scoping-adapter', input.roots);\n  const allowlist = new Set((input.allowlist ?? []).map(normalizePath));\n  const files = roots\n    .map((root) => resolveInsideWorkspace(workspaceRoot, root))\n    .filter((root) => workspacePathExists(root))\n    .flatMap((root) => collectTypeScriptFiles(root));\n  const snapshotFiles = filterFilesBySnapshot(files, workspaceRoot, input);\n  const program = createAnalysisProgram(snapshotFiles);\n  const units = snapshotFiles.map((file) => readSourceUnit(file, workspaceRoot, program));\n  const tenantEntities = collectTenantEntities(units);\n  const context: AnalysisContext = {\n    checker: program.getTypeChecker(),\n    tenantEntities,\n    tenantTables: new Set([...tenantEntities.values()].map((entity) => entity.tableName)),\n    includeRepositoryReadFindings: input.includeRepositoryReadFindings === true,\n  };\n  const result: AnalysisResult = {\n    observations: [],\n    findings: [],\n    readPaths: units.map((unit) => unit.relativePath),\n  };\n\n  for (const unit of units) {\n    analyzeTenantUnit(unit, result, allowlist, context);\n  }\n  analyzeGuardCoverage(units, result);\n\n  result.observations.sort(compareById);\n  result.findings.sort(compareById);\n  result.readPaths.sort();\n  // Measured (2026-08-13): the full-repo scan emits 11,471 observations\n  // (9,363 tenant_repository_call) — 5.84 MB of stdout that tripped the\n  // runner's parse cap and marked every night budget_exceeded. Findings\n  // are the actionable channel (66 rows); observations above the per-type\n  // cap are dropped DETERMINISTICALLY (post-sort, stable ids) and the\n  // drop is DECLARED per type — honest truncation, never silent.\n  const OBSERVATION_CAP_PER_TYPE = 3000;\n  const byType = new Map<string, number>();\n  const truncated: Record<string, number> = {};\n  const cappedObservations = result.observations.filter((observation) => {\n    const seen = (byType.get(observation.type) ?? 0) + 1;\n    byType.set(observation.type, seen);\n    if (seen > OBSERVATION_CAP_PER_TYPE) {\n      truncated[observation.type] = (truncated[observation.type] ?? 0) + 1;\n      return false;\n    }\n    return true;\n  });\n  const evidenceSources = Array.from(\n    new Set(result.findings.flatMap((finding) => finding.evidence.map((evidence) => evidence.path))),\n  ).sort();\n\n  return {\n    observations: cappedObservations,\n    findings: result.findings,\n    read_paths: Array.from(new Set(result.readPaths)).sort(),\n    evidence_sources: evidenceSources,\n    belief_candidates: [\n      {\n        belief_id: 'tenant-scoping:repository-boundary-surface',\n        claim: 'backend services use tenant context and repository/query boundaries that require tenant-scoped access checks',\n        confidence: 0.8,\n        evidence_refs: ['apps/**/*.ts', 'libs/backend-common/src/database/**/*.ts', 'platform/libs/**/*.ts'],\n        source_tool_id: 'tenant-scoping-adapter',\n      },\n    ],\n    cost_units: Array.from(new Set(result.readPaths)).length,\n    metadata: {\n      adapter: 'tenant-scoping-adapter',\n      roots: roots.map(String).sort(),\n      files_scanned: snapshotFiles.length,\n      findings_count: result.findings.length,\n      allowlist_count: allowlist.size,\n      tenant_owned_entity_count: tenantEntities.size,\n      // Declared truncation (see the cap above): counts of observations\n      // dropped per type. Empty object = nothing dropped.\n      observations_truncated: truncated,\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nfunction analyzeTenantUnit(\n  unit: SourceUnit,\n  result: AnalysisResult,\n  allowlist: ReadonlySet<string>,\n  context: AnalysisContext,\n): void {\n  const allowlisted = allowlist.has(unit.relativePath);\n  const hasTenantSignal = /\\btenantId\\b|@Tenant\\b|TenantGuard|tenantManagerRepo|TenantContext/.test(unit.text);\n  const guardNames = collectIdentifiers(unit.sourceFile).filter((name) => TENANT_GUARD_NAMES.has(name));\n\n  if (hasTenantSignal) {\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `tenant-source:${unit.relativePath}`,\n      type: 'tenant_source',\n      path: unit.relativePath,\n      line: 1,\n      details: {\n        hasTenantIdReference: /\\btenantId\\b/.test(unit.text),\n        hasTenantDecorator: /@Tenant\\b/.test(unit.text),\n        hasTenantManagerRepo: /tenantManagerRepo/.test(unit.text),\n        guardNames,\n      },\n    });\n  }\n\n  visit(unit.sourceFile, (node) => {\n    if (!ts.isCallExpression(node) || !ts.isPropertyAccessExpression(node.expression)) {\n      return;\n    }\n    const method = node.expression.name.text;\n    if (!REPOSITORY_METHODS.has(method) && !RAW_QUERY_METHODS.has(method)) {\n      return;\n    }\n    const line = lineOf(unit.sourceFile, node);\n    const enclosing = enclosingFunctionLike(node);\n    const scopeText = enclosing?.getText(unit.sourceFile) ?? unit.sourceFile.getText();\n    const callText = node.getText(unit.sourceFile);\n    const statementText = enclosingStatementText(node, unit.sourceFile);\n    const receiverText = node.expression.expression.getText(unit.sourceFile);\n    const boundary = classifyTenantBoundaryCall(node, unit, context);\n    if (!boundary.isBoundary) {\n      return;\n    }\n    const scopedByTenantManager = /tenantManagerRepo|tenantAware|tenantScoped|withTenant/i.test(receiverText + scopeText);\n    const callHasTenantPredicate = /\\btenantId\\b|tenant_id|current_tenant|set_config\\(['\"]app\\.current_tenant/.test(callText + statementText);\n    const scopeHasTenant = /\\btenantId\\b|@Tenant\\b|TenantContext|tenantManagerRepo/.test(scopeText);\n    const explicitCrossTenant = hasCrossTenantComment(unit.text, node.getFullStart(), node.getStart(unit.sourceFile));\n\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `tenant-repository-call:${unit.relativePath}:${line}:${method}`,\n      type: 'tenant_repository_call',\n      path: unit.relativePath,\n      line,\n      name: method,\n      details: {\n        receiver: receiverText,\n        scopeHasTenant,\n        callHasTenantPredicate,\n        scopedByTenantManager,\n        explicitCrossTenant,\n        allowlisted,\n        boundaryKind: boundary.kind,\n        entityName: boundary.entityName ?? null,\n        typeEvidence: boundary.typeEvidence,\n      },\n    });\n\n    if (\n      allowlisted\n      || explicitCrossTenant\n      || scopedByTenantManager\n      || unit.relativePath.includes('/migrations/')\n      || !scopeHasTenant\n      || callHasTenantPredicate\n      || !boundary.tenantOwned\n    ) {\n      return;\n    }\n    if (RAW_QUERY_METHODS.has(method)) {\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `tenant-raw-query-missing-predicate:${unit.relativePath}:${line}`,\n        rule: 'tenant_raw_query_missing_tenant_predicate',\n        severity: 'high',\n        path: unit.relativePath,\n        line,\n        message: 'Raw tenant-bound query is executed in a tenant-aware scope without an explicit tenant predicate.',\n        evidence: [{ path: unit.relativePath, line }],\n        confidence: isSeedOrBootstrapContext(unit.relativePath, scopeText) ? 0.55 : 0.9,\n        actionability: isSeedOrBootstrapContext(unit.relativePath, scopeText) ? 'review_required' : 'actionable',\n        review_reason: isSeedOrBootstrapContext(unit.relativePath, scopeText) ? 'seed_or_bootstrap_context' : undefined,\n        details: { method, receiver: receiverText, entityName: boundary.entityName ?? null },\n      });\n      return;\n    }\n    if (!inputAllowsRepositoryReadFinding(context, method)) {\n      return;\n    }\n    result.findings.push({\n      id: `tenant-repository-unscoped-read:${unit.relativePath}:${line}`,\n      rule: 'tenant_repository_unscoped_read',\n      severity: method === 'find' || method === 'findOne' || method === 'findOneBy' || method === 'findBy' ? 'medium' : 'high',\n      path: unit.relativePath,\n      line,\n      message: 'Repository call is made in a tenant-aware scope without an explicit tenant predicate or tenant-scoped repository helper.',\n      evidence: [{ path: unit.relativePath, line }],\n      confidence: 0.82,\n      actionability: 'actionable',\n      details: { method, receiver: receiverText, entityName: boundary.entityName ?? null },\n    });\n  });\n}\n\nfunction enclosingStatementText(node: ts.Node, sourceFile: ts.SourceFile): string {\n  let current: ts.Node | undefined = node;\n  while (current && !ts.isStatement(current)) {\n    current = current.parent;\n  }\n  return current?.getText(sourceFile) ?? node.getText(sourceFile);\n}\n\nfunction isSeedOrBootstrapContext(path: string, text: string): boolean {\n  return /seed|bootstrap|fixture/i.test(path) || /\\b(seed|bootstrap|fixture)\\b/i.test(text.slice(0, 4000));\n}\n\nfunction inputAllowsRepositoryReadFinding(context: AnalysisContext, method: string): boolean {\n  return context.includeRepositoryReadFindings || !['find', 'findOne', 'findOneBy', 'findBy', 'count'].includes(method);\n}\n\nfunction classifyTenantBoundaryCall(\n  node: ts.CallExpression,\n  unit: SourceUnit,\n  context: AnalysisContext,\n): {\n  readonly isBoundary: boolean;","numLines":320,"startLine":1,"totalLines":574}}}

 /**\n   * Login user - supports all roles including SUPER_ADMIN\n   *\n   * SECURITY:\n   * - Uses timing-safe operations to prevent timing attacks\n   * - Generic error messages to prevent user enumeration\n   * - Session management with concurrent session limits\n   */\n  async login(input: LoginInput, ipAddress?: string, userAgent?: string): Promise<AuthPayload> {\n    const startTime = Date.now();\n    // SECURITY: Do not log email addresses — PII under GDPR (SEC-AUTH-011)\n    this.logger.debug('Login attempt received');\n\n    try {\n      // Find user by email only (tenantId can be null for SUPER_ADMIN)\n      const user = await this.userRepository.findOne({\n        where: { email: input.email.toLowerCase() },\n      });\n\n      // SECURITY (SEC-HIGH-002): user-not-found runs the SAME peppered verify\n      // pipeline as a real user (applyPepper → bcrypt.compare) against a\n      // valid dummy hash — identical instruction sequence, no timing/path\n      // asymmetry that could leak account existence. The prior malformed\n      // dummy hash + skipped pepper HMAC was the enumeration vector.\n      if (!user) {\n        await verifyPassword(input.password, await this.getDummyPasswordHash());\n        await this.ensureMinDuration(startTime);\n        this.logger.debug(`Login failed: user not found`);\n        // SECURITY AUDIT: Log failed login attempt\n        await this.logSecurityEvent(\n          'LOGIN_FAILED',\n          {\n            email: input.email,\n            ipAddress,\n            userAgent,\n            success: false,\n            reason: 'User not found',\n          },\n          AuditLogSeverity.WARNING,\n        );\n        throw new UnauthorizedException(INVALID_CREDENTIALS_MSG);\n      }\n\n      // Check if user is pending invitation (no password set)\n      // SECURITY: Use generic message\n      if (user.isPendingInvitation()) {\n        await this.ensureMinDuration(startTime);\n        // SECURITY: Log user ID instead of email to prevent PII exposure (H-14)\n        this.logger.debug(`Login failed: pending invitation for userId=${user.id}`);\n        throw new UnauthorizedException(GENERIC_AUTH_ERROR_MSG);\n      }\n\n      // Check if account is locked\n      // SECURITY: Don't reveal lockout duration to prevent timing attacks\n      if (user.isLocked()) {\n        await this.ensureMinDuration(startTime);\n        // SECURITY: Log user ID instead of email to prevent PII exposure (H-14)\n        this.logger.debug(`Login failed: account locked for userId=${user.id}`);\n        // SECURITY AUDIT: Log locked account access attempt\n        await this.logSecurityEvent(\n          'LOGIN_BLOCKED_ACCOUNT_LOCKED',\n          {\n            userId: user.id,\n            email: user.email,\n            tenantId: user.tenantId,\n            ipAddress,\n            userAgent,\n            success: false,\n            reason: 'Account locked due to failed attempts',\n          },\n          AuditLogSeverity.WARNING,\n        );\n        throw new UnauthorizedException(GENERIC_AUTH_ERROR_MSG);\n      }\n\n      // Check if account is active\n      if (!user.isActive) {\n        await this.ensureMinDuration(startTime);\n        // SECURITY: Log user ID instead of email to prevent PII exposure (H-14)\n        this.logger.debug(`Login failed: account inactive for userId=${user.id}`);\n        throw new UnauthorizedException(GENERIC_AUTH_ERROR_MSG);\n      }\n\n      // SECURITY (MT-HIGH-003): only an ACTIVE tenant may authenticate.\n      // The previous check enumerated REJECTED statuses (SUSPENDED, CANCELLED),\n      // so DEACTIVATED, ARCHIVED, PENDING and PROVISIONING* tenants slipped\n      // through and could still log in. isLoginAllowed is the fail-closed\n      // allow-list (ACTIVE only) owned by the tenant-status machine — a new\n      // non-operational status is blocked by default, not by remembering to\n      // add it here. SUPER_ADMIN users (tenantId null) are exempt.\n      // The row is hoisted out of this block because it also drives the\n      // ADR-046 enforcement point below: the MFA-enrollment login gate.\n      let tenant: Tenant | null = null;\n      if (user.tenantId) {\n        tenant = await this.tenantRepository.findOne({ where: { id: user.tenantId } });\n        if (tenant && !isLoginAllowed(tenant.status)) {\n          await this.ensureMinDuration(startTime);\n          this.logger.debug(`Login failed: tenant ${user.tenantId} is ${tenant.status}`);\n          await this.logSecurityEvent(\n            'LOGIN_BLOCKED_TENANT_INACTIVE',\n            {\n              userId: user.id,\n              email: user.email,\n              tenantId: user.tenantId,\n              ipAddress,\n              userAgent,\n              success: false,\n              reason: `Tenant account status is ${tenant.status}`,\n            },\n            AuditLogSeverity.WARNING,\n          );\n          throw new UnauthorizedException('Tenant account is not active');\n        }\n      }\n\n      // Validate password\n      // SECURITY (HIGH-006): verifyPasswordAndSignalMigration returns a\n      // lazy-migration flag so legacy (unpeppered) hashes transparently\n      // upgrade to the HMAC-peppered format on successful login. We only\n      // re-hash AFTER all the other login-time checks (MFA, session cap)\n      // have cleared — see the block further down that persists the rehash.\n      const verifyResult = await user.verifyPasswordAndSignalMigration(input.password);\n      const isPasswordValid = verifyResult.matched;\n      const shouldMigratePasswordHash = verifyResult.shouldMigrate;\n\n      if (!isPasswordValid) {\n        // M-04: Use returned attempt count for accurate audit logging\n        const updatedAttempts = await this.handleFailedLogin(user);\n        await this.ensureMinDuration(startTime);\n        // SECURITY AUDIT: Log failed password attempt\n        await this.logSecurityEvent(\n          'LOGIN_FAILED_INVALID_PASSWORD',\n          {\n            userId: user.id,\n            email: user.email,\n            tenantId: user.tenantId,\n            ipAddress,\n            userAgent,\n            success: false,\n            reason: `Invalid password (attempt ${updatedAttempts})`,\n            // Carried on the signal so a brute-force detector reads a number\n            // instead of parsing it back out of the reason string.\n            failedAttempts: updatedAttempts,\n          },\n          AuditLogSeverity.WARNING,\n        );\n        throw new UnauthorizedException(INVALID_CREDENTIALS_MSG);\n      }\n\n      // Reset failed login attempts on successful password validation","numLines":150,"startLine":520,"totalLines":2189}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01AZdsW2hqH6qgMrQDbqmWUZ","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport { MigrationInterface, QueryRunner } from 'typeorm';\n2\t\n3\t/**\n4\t * DropRlsFromAuthUsersIdentity1787000000000\n5\t * ============================================================================\n6\t *\n7\t * Removes `tenant_isolation_policy` from `auth.users` and disables ROW LEVEL\n8\t * SECURITY on it, restoring the platform's original identity-primitive\n9\t * contract: `auth.users` is cross-tenant by design because every login flow\n10\t * reads from it BEFORE the tenant can be determined.\n11\t *\n12\t * # Incident this migration closes (DEPLOY-CRITICAL-006, 2026-04-21)\n13\t *\n14\t * The policy was installed by `RlsSchemaBootstrap` at auth-service boot,\n15\t * triggered by `RlsModule.forPoolService({ serviceName: 'auth', autoApply:\n16\t * true })` in app.module.ts. The module's own helper\n17\t * (`libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/tenant-rls-sync.service.ts:106-108`)\n18\t * explicitly warns against this:\n19\t *\n20\t *   \"auth-service — can't support RLS because of nullable tenantId on\n21\t *    SUPER_ADMIN rows. Don't register this service for those.\"\n22\t *\n23\t * Despite that, `autoApply: true` was committed and the bootstrap silently\n24\t * added `tenant_isolation_policy` to `auth.users`. Consequences:\n25\t *\n26\t *   1. Every login fails with \"User not found\" — because the login query\n27\t *      `findOne({ where: { email } })` runs with NO `app.current_tenant`\n28\t *      GUC set (tenant is DETERMINED by the user row, not queried with it).\n29\t *      The policy predicate `\"tenantId\" = NULLIF(current_setting(...),'')::uuid`\n30\t *      evaluates to UNKNOWN → 0 rows visible → \"User not found\".\n31\t *\n32\t *   2. SUPER_ADMIN users have `tenantId = NULL` by design. `NULL = <any uuid>`\n33\t *      is never TRUE, so platform administrators are STRUCTURALLY hidden\n34\t *      from the auth service regardless of GUC state.\n35\t *\n36\t * Evidence from the production droplet 2026-04-21 at 17:38–17:42 UTC:\n37\t *   - LOGIN_FAILED audit rows with reason=\"User not found\" for a user that\n38\t *     does exist (`okan@oceanfarm.eu`, `lastLoginAt = 2026-04-14`).\n39\t *   - Query as superuser `aquaculture` returns the row; query as\n40\t *     `auth_service` (the PG role auth-service connects with) returns 0 rows\n41\t *     on the same SELECT.\n42\t *   - `pg_policies` shows `auth | users | tenant_isolation_policy` active.\n43\t *\n44\t * # Why DROP and not \"bypass in login code\"\n45\t *\n46\t * This is a Tier-1 architectural correction, not a workaround. The policy\n47\t * was wrong because the table is cross-tenant by design — applying a\n48\t * tenant-isolation policy is a category error. Defense-in-depth for\n49\t * `auth.users` is correctly enforced out-of-band:\n50\t *\n51\t *   1. Schema-role isolation: only the `auth_service` PG role can access\n52\t *      `auth.*` (00-init-schemas.sh + per-service DB roles).\n53\t *   2. Application-layer tenant scoping: every post-auth query against\n54\t *      users (e.g. TenantAdminService.listTenantUsers) has explicit\n55\t *      `WHERE tenantId = ?` in code, verified by TenantGuard + e2e tests.\n56\t *   3. JWT-authenticated handlers: the only pre-auth query is the\n57\t *      login-by-email lookup itself; all other flows run with tenant\n58\t *      context from JwtAuthGuard.\n59\t *\n60\t * The companion code changes in the same commit:\n61\t *   - `apps/auth-service/src/app.module.ts`: adds `users`, `tenants` to\n62\t *     `excludeTables` so future `autoApply` passes skip identity primitives.\n63\t *   - `libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts`:\n64\t *     adds `DEFAULT_IDENTITY_TABLES = ['users', 'tenants']` that the helper\n65\t *     auto-skips regardless of caller config — Tier-1 \"make impossible\".\n66\t *\n67\t * # Idempotent\n68\t *\n69\t * `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` + `DISABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` are both no-ops\n70\t * when already applied, so the migration is safe to re-run in environments"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"66962382-f920-48a2-964d-9f75acea2f64","uuid":"025ee213-f860-4f98-970a-f6876f39eaeb","timestamp":"2026-09-21T01:33:04.580Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-214613d2c9c1/apps/auth-service/src/migrations/.archive/2026-05-18T09-35-46-244Z/1787000000000-DropRlsFromAuthUsersIdentity.ts","content":"import { MigrationInterface, QueryRunner } from 'typeorm';\n\n/**\n * DropRlsFromAuthUsersIdentity1787000000000\n * ============================================================================\n *\n * Removes `tenant_isolation_policy` from `auth.users` and disables ROW LEVEL\n * SECURITY on it, restoring the platform's original identity-primitive\n * contract: `auth.users` is cross-tenant by design because every login flow\n * reads from it BEFORE the tenant can be determined.\n *\n * # Incident this migration closes (DEPLOY-CRITICAL-006, 2026-04-21)\n *\n * The policy was installed by `RlsSchemaBootstrap` at auth-service boot,\n * triggered by `RlsModule.forPoolService({ serviceName: 'auth', autoApply:\n * true })` in app.module.ts. The module's own helper\n * (`libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/tenant-rls-sync.service.ts:106-108`)\n * explicitly warns against this:\n *\n *   \"auth-service — can't support RLS because of nullable tenantId on\n *    SUPER_ADMIN rows. Don't register this service for those.\"\n *\n * Despite that, `autoApply: true` was committed and the bootstrap silently\n * added `tenant_isolation_policy` to `auth.users`. Consequences:\n *\n *   1. Every login fails with \"User not found\" — because the login query\n *      `findOne({ where: { email } })` runs with NO `app.current_tenant`\n *      GUC set (tenant is DETERMINED by the user row, not queried with it).\n *      The policy predicate `\"tenantId\" = NULLIF(current_setting(...),'')::uuid`\n *      evaluates to UNKNOWN → 0 rows visible → \"User not found\".\n *\n *   2. SUPER_ADMIN users have `tenantId = NULL` by design. `NULL = <any uuid>`\n *      is never TRUE, so platform administrators are STRUCTURALLY hidden\n *      from the auth service regardless of GUC state.\n *\n * Evidence from the production droplet 2026-04-21 at 17:38–17:42 UTC:\n *   - LOGIN_FAILED audit rows with reason=\"User not found\" for a user that\n *     does exist (`okan@oceanfarm.eu`, `lastLoginAt = 2026-04-14`).\n *   - Query as superuser `aquaculture` returns the row; query as\n *     `auth_service` (the PG role auth-service connects with) returns 0 rows\n *     on the same SELECT.\n *   - `pg_policies` shows `auth | users | tenant_isolation_policy` active.\n *\n * # Why DROP and not \"bypass in login code\"\n *\n * This is a Tier-1 architectural correction, not a workaround. The policy\n * was wrong because the table is cross-tenant by design — applying a\n * tenant-isolation policy is a category error. Defense-in-depth for\n * `auth.users` is correctly enforced out-of-band:\n *\n *   1. Schema-role isolation: only the `auth_service` PG role can access\n *      `auth.*` (00-init-schemas.sh + per-service DB roles).\n *   2. Application-layer tenant scoping: every post-auth query against\n *      users (e.g. TenantAdminService.listTenantUsers) has explicit\n *      `WHERE tenantId = ?` in code, verified by TenantGuard + e2e tests.\n *   3. JWT-authenticated handlers: the only pre-auth query is the\n *      login-by-email lookup itself; all other flows run with tenant\n *      context from JwtAuthGuard.\n *\n * The companion code changes in the same commit:\n *   - `apps/auth-service/src/app.module.ts`: adds `users`, `tenants` to\n *     `excludeTables` so future `autoApply` passes skip identity primitives.\n *   - `libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts`:\n *     adds `DEFAULT_IDENTITY_TABLES = ['users', 'tenants']` that the helper\n *     auto-skips regardless of caller config — Tier-1 \"make impossible\".\n *\n * # Idempotent\n *\n * `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` + `DISABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` are both no-ops\n * when already applied, so the migration is safe to re-run in environments","numLines":70,"startLine":1,"totalLines":132}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01QoyFU334ZkjFRYNT2QBuCZ","type":"tool_result","content":"125\t *\n126\t * Defense-in-depth on these tables is enforced OUT-OF-BAND:\n127\t *   1. Schema-role isolation (`auth_service` PG role is the only client).\n128\t *   2. Application-layer explicit `WHERE tenantId = ?` on all post-auth\n129\t *      tenant-scoped queries.\n130\t *   3. JWT-authenticated handlers with TenantGuard enforce tenant context.\n131\t *\n132\t * The helper auto-skips these table names in any schema it sweeps. This\n133\t * lives at the helper layer (not only at the caller's `excludeTables`)\n134\t * because the invariant is platform-wide: no identity primitive in ANY\n135\t * schema may be RLS-protected by tenant_isolation_policy. Tier-1 \"make\n136\t * impossible\" — a new service that accidentally uses `autoApply: true`\n137\t * against an auth-like schema cannot re-introduce the 2026-04-21 login\n138\t * outage. Logged at WARN so the skip is greppable in deploy audits.\n139\t *\n140\t * To install per-tenant isolation on a table that HAPPENS to share one of\n141\t * these names (highly unusual), the caller must pass an explicit\n142\t * `allowIdentityTables: true` option (not yet exposed — add with care\n143\t * and an ADR if a legitimate case ever arises).\n144\t */\n145\texport const DEFAULT_IDENTITY_TABLES = ['users', 'tenants'] as const;\n146\t\n147\t/** The single, canonical policy name. JWT-authenticated handlers with TenantGuard enforce tenant context.\n *\n * The helper auto-skips these table names in any schema it sweeps. This\n * lives at the helper layer (not only at the caller's `excludeTables`)\n * because the invariant is platform-wide: no identity primitive in ANY\n * schema may be RLS-protected by tenant_isolation_policy. Tier-1 \"make\n * impossible\" — a new service that accidentally uses `autoApply: true`\n * against an auth-like schema cannot re-introduce the 2026-04-21 login\n * outage. Derived by\n79\t    # `plan_origin.commit_contract_for_plan`, REQUIRED of role=implementation\n80\t    # (checked below), absent on every other role.\n81\t    \"commit_contract\",\n82\t)\n83\t\n84\tRESPONSE_REQUIRED_FIELDS = (\n85\t    \"$schema\",\n86\t    \"request_id\",\n87\t    \"claim_id\",\n88\t    \"agent_id\",\n89\t    \"role\",\n90\t    \"status\",\n91\t    \"satisfaction_matrix\",\n92\t)\n93\t\n94\tRESPONSE_STATUSES = (\"submitted\", \"accepted\", \"rejected\", \"partial\")\n95\t\n96\tSATISFACTION_VERDICTS = (\"satisfied\", \"blocked\", \"contradicted\")\n97\t\n98\tREASON_CLASSES = (\"law\", \"scope\", \"evidence\", \"safety\")\n99\t\n100\tREQUEST_SCHEMA = \"aria/agent-request/v1\"\n101\t\n102\t# ARIA-HIGH-104 — the roles whose row the queue refuses to append unless\n103\t# `validate_request` accepts it (with the store, so the plan-revision\n104\t# agreement check runs). A declared set rather than a per-call flag, so a\n105\t# minter cannot forget to opt in.\n106\t#\n107\t# The boundary of the set is what this contract BINDS: a request to the\n108\t# cycle it runs in (`cycle_id`, REQUIRED above) and to the plan revision it\n109\t# works from (`convergence_id` + `plan_revision_hash`, whose derived\n110\t# validation suite and commit contract the envelope must equal). A role\n111\t# belongs here when every production mint of it has both. The\n112\t# implementation role does: it is minted by the cycle's implementation\n113\t# phase from a CONVERGED revision. The operator-driven judge, curation,\n114\t# questioning and self-change lanes run in no cycle and implement no plan\n115\t# revision, so there is nothing for this contract to bind them to; their\n116\t# rows are held by the queue's own field validation at mint\n117\t# (`validate_must_satisfy`, scope, evidence refs) and by `validate_response`\n118\t# at submit, and their envelopes still carry the derived\n119\t# `validation_commands` and `forbidden_scope` fields."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"66962382-f920-48a2-964d-9f75acea2f64","uuid":"3cdabea4-c956-4d4b-a198-86c503a03c6e","timestamp":"2026-09-21T01:34:29.143Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-214613d2c9c1/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/agent_contract.py","content":"    \"validation_commands\",\n    \"expected_output_path\",\n)\n\nREQUEST_OPTIONAL_FIELDS = (\n    \"pressure_event_id\",\n    \"plan_id\",\n    \"converged_plan_hash\",\n    \"impact_graph_refs\",\n    \"repository_map\",\n    \"context_source_paths\",\n    \"context_source_paths_status\",\n    \"separation_of_duties\",\n    \"round_number\",\n    \"created_at\",\n    \"plan_contract\",\n    # ARIA-HIGH-104 (4) — the commit contract an implementation envelope\n    # carries: the trailer the plan's origin admits (or none) and the commit\n    # types the commit-msg gate accepts without one. Derived by\n    # `plan_origin.commit_contract_for_plan`, REQUIRED of role=implementation\n    # (checked below), absent on every other role.\n    \"commit_contract\",\n)\n\nRESPONSE_REQUIRED_FIELDS = (\n    \"$schema\",\n    \"request_id\",\n    \"claim_id\",\n    \"agent_id\",\n    \"role\",\n    \"status\",\n    \"satisfaction_matrix\",\n)\n\nRESPONSE_STATUSES = (\"submitted\", \"accepted\", \"rejected\", \"partial\")\n\nSATISFACTION_VERDICTS = (\"satisfied\", \"blocked\", \"contradicted\")\n\nREASON_CLASSES = (\"law\", \"scope\", \"evidence\", \"safety\")\n\nREQUEST_SCHEMA = \"aria/agent-request/v1\"\n\n# ARIA-HIGH-104 — the roles whose row the queue refuses to append unless\n# `validate_request` accepts it (with the store, so the plan-revision\n# agreement check runs). A declared set rather than a per-call flag, so a\n# minter cannot forget to opt in.\n#\n# The boundary of the set is what this contract BINDS: a request to the\n# cycle it runs in (`cycle_id`, REQUIRED above) and to the plan revision it\n# works from (`convergence_id` + `plan_revision_hash`, whose derived\n# validation suite and commit contract the envelope must equal). A role\n# belongs here when every production mint of it has both. The\n# implementation role does: it is minted by the cycle's implementation\n# phase from a CONVERGED revision. The operator-driven judge, curation,\n# questioning and self-change lanes run in no cycle and implement no plan\n# revision, so there is nothing for this contract to bind them to; their\n# rows are held by the queue's own field validation at mint\n# (`validate_must_satisfy`, scope, evidence refs) and by `validate_response`\n# at submit, and their envelopes still carry the derived\n# `validation_commands` and `forbidden_scope` fields.","numLines":60,"startLine":60,"totalLines":616}}}

   *,\n    request: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n    lease: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Validate an `aria/agent-response/v1` envelope.\n\n    `request` is optional — when provided, the response is cross-checked\n    against the request's `must_satisfy` ids and `expected_output_path`.\n\n    `lease` is optional — when provided (Plan 023 v3 §A-5), the\n    envelope's claim_id and agent_id MUST match the leased identity.\n    Pre-Plan-023 the envelope's claim_id was only validated for SHAPE\n    (CLAIM_ID_RE pattern) and the request_id was matched to the\n    request, but envelope.claim_id and envelope.agent_id were never\n    bound to the lease that authorized the submission. A stitched\n    envelope from a different lease (same request_id but different\n    claim/agent) would pass.\n    \"\"\"\n    missing = [f for f in RESPONSE_REQUIRED_FIELDS if f not in envelope]\n    if missing:\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response missing required fields: {missing}\")\n    if envelope[\"$schema\"] != RESPONSE_SCHEMA:\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response $schema must be {RESPONSE_SCHEMA!r}\")\n    if not isinstance(envelope[\"request_id\"], str) or not REQUEST_ID_RE.match(envelope[\"request_id\"]):\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.request_id invalid: {envelope['request_id']!r}\")\n    claim_id = envelope[\"claim_id\"]\n    if not isinstance(claim_id, str) or not CLAIM_ID_RE.match(claim_id):\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.claim_id invalid: {claim_id!r}\")\n    agent_id = envelope[\"agent_id\"]\n    if not isinstance(agent_id, str) or not AGENT_ID_RE.match(agent_id):\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.agent_id invalid: {agent_id!r}\")\n    role = envelope[\"role\"]\n    if role not in INVOCATION_ROLES:\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.role unknown: {role!r}\")\n    status = envelope[\"status\"]\n    if status not in RESPONSE_STATUSES:\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"agent-response.status unknown: {status!r}\")\n\n    matrix = envelope[\"satisfaction_matrix\"]\n    if not isinstance(matrix, list):\n        raise GovernanceError(\"agent-response.satisfaction_matrix must be a list\")\n    matrix_entries: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    matrix_ids: set[str] = set()\n    for idx, entry in enumerate(matrix):\n        if not isinstance(entry, dict):\n            raise GovernanceError(f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}] must be an object\")\n        item_id = entry.get(\"id\")\n        verdict = entry.get(\"verdict\")\n        if not isinstance(item_id, str) or not item_id.strip():\n            raise GovernanceError(f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}].id is required\")\n        if item_id in matrix_ids:\n            raise GovernanceError(f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}].id duplicate: {item_id}\")\n        matrix_ids.add(item_id)\n        if verdict not in SATISFACTION_VERDICTS:\n            raise GovernanceError(\n                f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}].verdict {verdict!r} not in {SATISFACTION_VERDICTS}\"\n            )\n        if verdict in {\"blocked\", \"contradicted\"}:\n            note = entry.get(\"note\") or entry.get(\"rationale\") or \"\"\n            if not isinstance(note, str) or not note.strip():\n                raise GovernanceError(\n                    f\"satisfaction_matrix[{idx}].note required when verdict={verdict!r}\"\n                )\n            _check_banned_phrases(note, field=f\"satisfaction_matrix[{item_id}].note\")\n            evidence_refs = entry.get(\"evidence_refs\", [])\n            _ensure_string_list(\n                evidence_refs,\n                field=f\"satisfaction_matrix[{item_id}].evidence_refs\",\n                allow_empty=False,\n            )\n        matrix_entries.append(entry)\n\n    rationale = envelope.get(\"rationale\")\n    if isinstance(rationale, str) and rationale.strip():\n        _check_banned_phrases(rationale, field=\"agent-response.rationale\")\n\n    # Plan 023 v3 §A-5 — envelope claim/agent identity bound to lease.\n    # When the caller supplies the leased claim+agent identity (which\n    # submit_claim_result does), reject any envelope whose claim_id or\n    # agent_id differs. Pre-fix a stitched envelope from a different\n    # lease (same request_id but different claim/agent) passed because\n    # only request_id was cross-checked.\n    if lease is not None:\n        leased_claim = lease.get(\"claim_id\")\n        leased_agent = lease.get(\"agent_id\")\n        if leased_claim is not None and envelope[\"claim_id\"] != leased_claim:\n            raise GovernanceError(\n                f\"envelope_claim_id_mismatch: envelope.claim_id=\"\n                f\"{envelope['claim_id']!r} does not match leased claim_id=\"\n                f\"{leased_claim!r}\"\n            )\n        if leased_agent is not None and envelope[\"agent_id\"] != leased_agent:\n            raise GovernanceError(\n                f\"envelope_agent_id_mismatch: envelope.agent_id=\"\n                f\"{envelope['agent_id']!r} does not match leased agent_id=\"\n                f\"{leased_agent!r}\"\n            )\n\n    if request is not None:\n        if envelope[\"request_id\"] != request.get(\"request_id\"):","numLines":100,"startLine":365,"totalLines":616}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018jYq1FAU7eyWG3J5CWWq4M","type":"tool_result","content":"2290\t    #\n2291\t    # WHY: claude -p stream-json emits JSONL events\n2292\t    # ({\"result\": \"...\", \"total_cost_usd\": ..., ...}) where the agent's final message may contain the\n2293\t    # actual aria/agent-response/v1 envelope as fenced ```json``` block\n2294\t    # or as final JSON block. Writing raw JSONL to output_path means\n2295\t    # kernel submit-result reads runtime telemetry instead of the envelope,\n2296\t    # which fails:\n2297\t    #   response_schema: missing required fields:\n2298\t    #     ['$schema', 'request_id', 'claim_id', 'agent_id', 'role',\n2299\t    #      'status', 'satisfaction_matrix']\n2300\t    #\n2301\t    # HOW: parse JSONL, extract the final message, find the embedded\n2302\t    # envelope JSON, INJECT mandatory identity fields ($schema,\n2303\t    # request_id, claim_id, agent_id, role, status) from the known\n2304\t    # ci_executor context (these aren't the agent's job — the agent\n2305\t    # only knows its plan content), synthesize a satisfaction_matrix\n2306\t    # from must_satisfy with verdict=satisfied when the agent omitted\n2307\t    # it, then write the corrected envelope to output_path.\n2308\t    #\n2309\t    # Tier hierarchy: Tier-2 (Make it automatic) — the envelope shape\n2310\t    # is now produced correctly by default; agents don't have to know\n2311\t    # internal kernel identity fields.\n2312\t    if completed.stdout:\n2313\t        envelope = _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n2314\t            raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n2315\t            request_id=request_id,\n2316\t            claim_id=claim_id or \"\",\n2317\t            agent_id=agent_id or \"\",\n2318\t            role=role,\n2319\t            subagent_type=subagent_type,\n2320\t            must_satisfy=must_satisfy or [],\n2321\t            # ARIA-MEDIUM-171 — the rung that answered, not the frontmatter.\n2322\t            dispatch_model=completed.model or agent_profile.model,\n2323\t            usage=completed.usage,\n2324\t        )\n2325\t        envelope[\"details\"][\"agent_contract_hash\"] = agent_contract.contract_hash\n2326\t        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n2327\t        _write_sanitized_envelope(output_path, envelope)\n2328\t        resolved_transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n2329\t        resolved_transcript_path.write_text(completed.stdout, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n2330\t        # Plan ARIA-V3.1-D3 — per-LLM-call cost attribution. Gated on\n2331\t        # _MOCK_MODE_AT_ENTRY frozen sentinel (V3.1-D2) so a mid-run\n2332\t        # CLAUDE_CLI_MOCK flip cannot rewrite mock-mode classification\n2333\t        # between mint + record sites. request_envelope + tools_dir\n2334\t        # MUST both be supplied for the record to fire — None defaults"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"66962382-f920-48a2-964d-9f75acea2f64","uuid":"293f9817-4c64-4c80-af08-fdabebe80b3b","timestamp":"2026-09-21T01:34:40.887Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-214613d2c9c1/tools/aria-poc/ci_executor.py","content":"    #\n    # WHY: claude -p stream-json emits JSONL events\n    # ({\"result\": \"...\", \"total_cost_usd\": ..., ...}) where the agent's final message may contain the\n    # actual aria/agent-response/v1 envelope as fenced ```json``` block\n    # or as final JSON block. Writing raw JSONL to output_path means\n    # kernel submit-result reads runtime telemetry instead of the envelope,\n    # which fails:\n    #   response_schema: missing required fields:\n    #     ['$schema', 'request_id', 'claim_id', 'agent_id', 'role',\n    #      'status', 'satisfaction_matrix']\n    #\n    # HOW: parse JSONL, extract the final message, find the embedded\n    # envelope JSON, INJECT mandatory identity fields ($schema,\n    # request_id, claim_id, agent_id, role, status) from the known\n    # ci_executor context (these aren't the agent's job — the agent\n    # only knows its plan content), synthesize a satisfaction_matrix\n    # from must_satisfy with verdict=satisfied when the agent omitted\n    # it, then write the corrected envelope to output_path.\n    #\n    # Tier hierarchy: Tier-2 (Make it automatic) — the envelope shape\n    # is now produced correctly by default; agents don't have to know\n    # internal kernel identity fields.\n    if completed.stdout:\n        envelope = _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n            raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n            request_id=request_id,\n            claim_id=claim_id or \"\",\n            agent_id=agent_id or \"\",\n            role=role,\n            subagent_type=subagent_type,\n            must_satisfy=must_satisfy or [],\n            # ARIA-MEDIUM-171 — the rung that answered, not the frontmatter.\n            dispatch_model=completed.model or agent_profile.model,\n            usage=completed.usage,\n        )\n        envelope[\"details\"][\"agent_contract_hash\"] = agent_contract.contract_hash\n        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        _write_sanitized_envelope(output_path, envelope)\n        resolved_transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        resolved_transcript_path.write_text(completed.stdout, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        # Plan ARIA-V3.1-D3 — per-LLM-call cost attribution. Gated on\n        # _MOCK_MODE_AT_ENTRY frozen sentinel (V3.1-D2) so a mid-run\n        # CLAUDE_CLI_MOCK flip cannot rewrite mock-mode classification\n        # between mint + record sites. request_envelope + tools_dir\n        # MUST both be supplied for the record to fire — None defaults","numLines":45,"startLine":2290,"totalLines":5532}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KGtDs2XccyZtGhshciyouL","type":"tool_result","content":"2584\tdef _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n2585\t    *,\n2586\t    raw_stdout: str,\n2587\t    request_id: str,\n2588\t    claim_id: str,\n2589\t    agent_id: str,\n2590\t    role: str,\n2591\t    subagent_type: str,\n2592\t    must_satisfy: list[dict[str, Any]],\n2593\t    dispatch_model: str | None = None,\n2594\t    usage: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n2595\t    confidence_source: str = \"self_reported\",\n2596\t) -> dict[str, Any]:\n2597\t    \"\"\"Convert ``claude -p stream-json`` JSONL into a kernel-valid envelope.\n2598\t\n2599\t    ``confidence_source`` is the ROUTE's word on where a judge's confidence\n2600\t    came from (typed-judgment plan, ARIA-HIGH-167): every CLI and chat\n2601\t    completion in the fleet reports the model's own number\n2602\t    (``self_reported``); a typed decision transport that returns\n2603\t    probabilities natively stamps ``provider_reported``. Stamped\n2604\t    unconditionally, like the dispatch model: the bridge reads the stamp and\n2605\t    ignores any spelling the model wrote inside its payload.\n2606\t\n2607\t    ``usage`` is the run result's own usage block when the caller holds one\n2608\t    (ARIA-HIGH-161): the Z.ai transport reports usage on the result, not as\n2609\t    a stream-json event, so parsing ``raw_stdout`` alone left the\n2610\t    cross-vendor rung's envelope without ``claude_cli_usage`` and the native\n2611\t    wrapper released a finished verdict as ``usage_unavailable``. The stream\n2612\t    parse stays the fallback for a caller that has only the bytes.\n2613\t\n2614\t    Claude Code emits JSONL events. ARIA keeps those raw events out of\n2615\t    artifacts, extracts the final agent message, and injects the\n2616\t    lease-bound identity fields that the agent cannot know.\n2617\t    \"\"\"\n2618\t    events = parse_claude_jsonl(raw_stdout)\n2619\t    agent_text = extract_final_message(events)\n2620\t    if not agent_text:\n2621\t        agent_text = raw_stdout\n2622\t    extracted = _extract_envelope_json(agent_text) or {}\n2623\t\n2624\t    envelope: dict[str, Any] = {\n2625\t        \"$schema\": \"aria/agent-response/v1\",\n2626\t        \"request_id\": request_id,\n2627\t        \"claim_id\": claim_id,\n2628\t        \"agent_id\": agent_id,\n2629\t        \"role\": role,\n2630\t        \"status\": str(extracted.get(\"status\") or \"submitted\"),\n2631\t    }\n2632\t\n2633\t    matrix_in = extracted.get(\"satisfaction_matrix\")\n2634\t    if isinstance(matrix_in, list) and matrix_in:\n2635\t        envelope[\"satisfaction_matrix\"] = matrix_in\n2636\t    else:\n2637\t        # Synthesize from must_satisfy so the kernel's non-empty-matrix\n2638\t        # check passes — as UNVERIFIED rows (ARIA-AUDIT-024). The agent\n2639\t        # being invoked and replying is transport evidence, not a\n2640\t        # satisfaction judgment; the verdict belongs to an independent\n2641\t        # judge reading this matrix, never to the executor that carried\n2642\t        # the reply.\n2643\t        synthesized: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n2644\t        excerpt = (agent_text or \"<agent produced no text>\").strip()\n2645\t        excerpt_short = excerpt[:240] + (\"...\" if len(excerpt) > 240 else \"\")\n2646\t        if must_satisfy:\n2647\t            for item in must_satisfy:\n2648\t                if not isinstance(item, dict):\n2649\t                    continue\n2650\t                cid = item.get(\"id\")\n2651\t                if not cid:\n2652\t                    continue\n2653\t                synthesized.append({\n2654\t                    \"id\": cid,\n2655\t                    \"verdict\": \"unverified\",\n2656\t                    \"evidence_refs\": [],\n2657\t                    \"evidence\": excerpt_short,\n2658\t                })\n2659\t        if not synthesized:\n2660\t            # Last-resort single-row matrix; agent_text is the only\n2661\t            # truthful evidence. Without this, the kernel rejects with\n2662\t            # evidence_satisfaction_matrix_must_be_non_empty.\n2663\t            synthesized.append({\n2664\t                \"id\": f\"agent-text-{request_id[-8:]}\",\n2665\t                \"verdict\": \"unverified\",\n2666\t                \"evidence_refs\": [],\n2667\t                \"evidence\": excerpt_short,\n2668\t            })\n2669\t        envelope[\"satisfaction_matrix\"] = synthesized\n2670\t\n2671\t    # Carry through any agent-supplied evidence_refs / details / notes.\n2672\t    for passthrough in (\"evidence_refs\", \"details\", \"notes\", \"plan_content\"):\n2673\t        if passthrough in extracted and extracted[passthrough] is not None:\n2674\t            envelope[passthrough] = extracted[passthrough]\n2675\t    if \"evidence_refs\" not in envelope:\n2676\t        envelope[\"evidence_refs\"] = []\n2677\t\n2678\t    # Embed the raw agent text under details so operators have full\n2679\t    # forensic context post-submission.\n2680\t    details = envelope.get(\"details\")\n2681\t    if not isinstance(details, dict):\n2682\t        details = {}\n2683\t    # D1 (Kapalı Döngü) — FORCE-set, not setdefault: this field is now the\n2684\t    # judge's calibration identity (judgment_bridge reads it as judge_id),\n2685\t    # and a setdefault would let the agent's own payload spoof another\n2686\t    # judge's identity into the per-judge precision ledger.\n2687\t    details[\"agent_subagent_type\"] = subagent_type\n2688\t    # ORPHAN-HIGH-781 — the dispatch-resolved model, same doctrine as\n2689\t    # agent_subagent_type above: FORCE-set from the runtime profile the\n2690\t    # executor resolved at invocation time. The judge's own\n2691\t    # verdict.model self-report remains in the payload as data, but the\n2692\t    # kernel's anchor distinct-model count must not trust a string the\n2693\t    # judged agent wrote about itself — a misreported model silently\n2694\t    # satisfies or violates ANCHOR_MIN_DISTINCT_MODELS, which is exactly\n2695\t    # the guarantee that field exists to provide.\n2696\t    # ARIA-MEDIUM-171 — the route's word, never the agent's: a named model\n2697\t    # overwrites whatever the agent wrote under the key, and a route that\n2698\t    # named nothing REMOVES the key rather than leaving the agent's spelling\n2699\t    # in place (ORPHAN-HIGH-781 pinned \"no stale stamp\" for the absent case).\n2700\t    if dispatch_model:\n2701\t        details[\"agent_dispatch_model\"] = dispatch_model\n2702\t    else:\n2703\t        details.pop(\"agent_dispatch_model\", None)\n2704\t    details[\"agent_confidence_source\"] = confidence_source\n2705\t    details.setdefault(\"agent_text\", _safe_agent_text_excerpt(agent_text))\n2706\t    if usage is None:\n2707\t        usage = extract_usage(parse_claude_jsonl(raw_stdout))\n2708\t    if usage is not None:\n2709\t        details.setdefault(\"claude_cli_usage\", usage)\n2710\t    envelope[\"details\"] = details\n2711\t\n2712\t    return envelope\n2713\t\n2714\t\n2715\tdef _rejected_result_recorded(submit_stdout: str) -> bool:\n2716\t    \"\"\"True when the submit step's answer is a kernel-recorded REJECTED result row.\n2717\t\n2718\t    `aria_kernel agent submit-result` prints its result document on stdout and\n2719\t    exits 1 for anything but `accepted`. When that document carries a result\n2720\t    row with status `rejected`, the kernel has already appended the row: the\n2721\t    claim is terminal (`_assert_lifecycle_mutation_allowed`) and the request\n2722\t    derives REJECTED (`derive_request_state`, results dominate). Anything else\n2723\t    on a non-zero exit is a refusal before the row and leaves the claim live.\n2724\t    \"\"\"\n2725\t    text = submit_stdout or \"\"\n2726\t    start = text.find(\"{\")\n2727\t    if start < 0:\n2728\t        return False\n2729\t    try:\n2730\t        payload = json.loads(text[start:])\n2731\t    except ValueError:\n2732\t        return False\n2733\t    row = payload.get(\"row\") if isinstance(payload, dict) else None\n2734\t    return (isinstance(payload, dict) and payload.get(\"status\") == \"rejected\"\n2735\t            and isinstance(row, dict) and row.get(\"row_type\") == \"result\" and row.get(\"status\") == \"rejected\")\n2736\t\n2737\t\n2738\tdef _held_request_state(request_id: str, base_dir: Path) -> str | None:\n2739\t    \"\"\"The lease guard's question to the kernel: what state is the request in?\n2740\t\n2741\t    None when the kernel has no such request — nothing can be held, so the\n2742\t    guard has nothing to hand back (the mocked executor fixtures claim a\n2743\t    request that was never minted). `derive_request_state` refuses an\n2744\t    unknown id, and a refusal the guard could not tell from an unreadable\n2745\t    ledger would make it release on every mocked run.\n2746\t    \"\"\"\n2747\t    from aria_kernel.agent_invocations import list_agent_invocation_requests\n2748\t\n2749\t    if not list_agent_invocation_requests(request_id=request_id, base_dir=base_dir):\n2750\t        return None\n2751\t    return _derive_request_state(request_id=request_id, base_dir=base_dir)\n2752\t\n2753\t\n2754\tdef _rejected_only_for_verification_unavailable(submit_stdout: str) -> bool:\n2755\t    \"\"\"Did the kernel reject the submission SOLELY because it could not verify?\n2756\t\n2757\t    The kernel CLI prints its verdict as JSON on stdout: on a rejection,\n2758\t    ``reasons`` (prose, for the ledger and the operator) and\n2759\t    ``rejection_codes`` (one machine code per reason). This reads the codes\n2760\t    — never the prose — and answers True only when every code says the\n2761\t    kernel's evidence probe could not run (git did not answer inside its\n2762\t    bound on a loaded host). That is the harness's gap, not the work's: the\n2763\t    same envelope resubmitted on a quiet host usually verifies, and it must\n2764\t    not burn the request's requeue budget the way `submit_rejected` does\n2765\t    (the 2026-08-09 `baseline_unavailable` class, at the acceptance seam).\n2766\t\n2767\t    Anything else — unparseable output, a kernel exception, no codes, or\n2768\t    ONE code that is about the work — is the request's fault: fail toward\n2769\t    the human, never toward silent infinite retry.\n2770\t    \"\"\"\n2771\t    try:\n2772\t        payload = json.loads(submit_stdout or \"\")\n2773\t    except ValueError:\n2774\t        return False\n2775\t    if not isinstance(payload, dict) or payload.get(\"status\") != \"rejected\":\n2776\t        return False\n2777\t    codes = payload.get(\"rejection_codes\")\n2778\t    if not isinstance(codes, list) or not codes:\n2779\t        return False\n2780\t    return all(\n2781\t        isinstance(code, str) and code in _EVIDENCE_VERIFICATION_UNAVAILABLE_CODES\n2782\t        for code in codes\n2783\t _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n    *,\n    raw_stdout: str,\n    request_id: str,\n    claim_id: str,\n    agent_id: str,\n    role: str,\n    subagent_type: str,\n    must_satisfy: list[dict[str, Any]],\n    dispatch_model: str | None = None,\n    usage: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n    confidence_source: str = \"self_reported\",\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Convert ``claude -p stream-json`` JSONL into a kernel-valid envelope.\n\n    ``confidence_source`` is the ROUTE's word on where a judge's confidence\n    came from (typed-judgment plan, ARIA-HIGH-167): every CLI and chat\n    completion in the fleet reports the model's own number\n    (``self_reported``); a typed decision transport that returns\n    probabilities natively stamps ``provider_reported``. Stamped\n    unconditionally, like the dispatch model: the bridge reads the stamp and\n    ignores any spelling the model wrote inside its payload.\n\n    ``usage`` is the run result's own usage block when the caller holds one\n    (ARIA-HIGH-161): the Z.ai transport reports usage on the result, not as\n    a stream-json event, so parsing ``raw_stdout`` alone left the\n    cross-vendor rung's envelope without ``claude_cli_usage`` and the native\n    wrapper released a finished verdict as ``usage_unavailable``. The stream\n    parse stays the fallback for a caller that has only the bytes.\n\n    Claude Code emits JSONL events. ARIA keeps those raw events out of\n    artifacts, extracts the final agent message, and injects the\n    lease-bound identity fields that the agent cannot know.\n    \"\"\"\n    events = parse_claude_jsonl(raw_stdout)\n    agent_text = extract_final_message(events)\n    if not agent_text:\n        agent_text = raw_stdout\n    extracted = _extract_envelope_json(agent_text) or {}\n\n    envelope: dict[str, Any] = {\n        \"$schema\": \"aria/agent-response/v1\",\n        \"request_id\": request_id,\n        \"claim_id\": claim_id,\n        \"agent_id\": agent_id,\n        \"role\": role,\n        \"status\": str(extracted.get(\"status\") or \"submitted\"),\n    }\n\n    matrix_in = extracted.get(\"satisfaction_matrix\")\n    if isinstance(matrix_in, list) and matrix_in:\n        envelope[\"satisfaction_matrix\"] = matrix_in\n    else:\n        # Synthesize from must_satisfy so the kernel's non-empty-matrix\n        # check passes — as UNVERIFIED rows (ARIA-AUDIT-024). The agent\n        # being invoked and replying is transport evidence, not a\n        # satisfaction judgment; the verdict belongs to an independent\n        # judge reading this matrix, never to the executor that carried\n        # the reply.\n        synthesized: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n        excerpt = (agent_text or \"<agent produced no text>\").strip()\n        excerpt_short = excerpt[:240] + (\"...\" if len(excerpt) > 240 else \"\")\n        if must_satisfy:\n            for item in must_satisfy:\n                if not isinstance(item, dict):\n                    continue\n                cid = item.get(\"id\")\n                if not cid:\n                    continue\n                synthesized.append({\n                    \"id\": cid,\n                    \"verdict\": \"unverified\",\n                    \"evidence_refs\": [],\n                    \"evidence\": excerpt_short,\n                })\n        if not synthesized:\n            # Last-resort single-row matrix; agent_text is the only\n            # truthful evidence. Without this, the kernel rejects with\n            # evidence_satisfaction_matrix_must_be_non_empty.\n            synthesized.append({\n                \"id\": f\"agent-text-{request_id[-8:]}\",\n                \"verdict\": \"unverified\",\n                \"evidence_refs\": [],\n                \"evidence\": excerpt_short,\n            })\n        envelope[\"satisfaction_matrix\"] = synthesized\n\n    # Carry through any agent-supplied evidence_refs / details / notes.\n    for passthrough in (\"evidence_refs\", \"details\", \"notes\", \"plan_content\"):\n        if passthrough in extracted and extracted[passthrough] is not None:\n            envelope[passthrough] = extracted[passthrough]\n    if \"evidence_refs\" not in envelope:\n        envelope[\"evidence_refs\"] = []\n\n    # Embed the raw agent text under details so operators have full\n    # forensic context post-submission.\n    details = envelope.get(\"details\")\n    if not isinstance(details, dict):\n        details = {}\n    # D1 (Kapalı Döngü) — FORCE-set, not setdefault: this field is now the\n    # judge's calibration identity (judgment_bridge reads it as judge_id),\n    # and a setdefault would let the agent's own payload spoof another\n    # judge's identity into the per-judge precision ledger.\n    details[\"agent_subagent_type\"] = subagent_type\n    # ORPHAN-HIGH-781 — the dispatch-resolved model, same doctrine as\n    # agent_subagent_type above: FORCE-set from the runtime profile the\n    # executor resolved at invocation time. The judge's own\n    # verdict.model self-report remains in the payload as data, but the\n    # kernel's anchor distinct-model count must not trust a string the\n    # judged agent wrote about itself — a misreported model silently\n    # satisfies or violates ANCHOR_MIN_DISTINCT_MODELS, which is exactly\n    # the guarantee that field exists to provide.\n    # ARIA-MEDIUM-171 — the route's word, never the agent's: a named model\n    # overwrites whatever the agent wrote under the key, and a route that\n    # named nothing REMOVES the key rather than leaving the agent's spelling\n    # in place (ORPHAN-HIGH-781 pinned \"no stale stamp\" for the absent case).\n    if dispatch_model:\n        details[\"agent_dispatch_model\"] = dispatch_model\n    else:\n        details.pop(\"agent_dispatch_model\", None)\n    details[\"agent_confidence_source\"] = confidence_source\n    details.setdefault(\"agent_text\", _safe_agent_text_excerpt(agent_text))\n    if usage is None:\n        usage = extract_usage(parse_claude_jsonl(raw_stdout))\n    if usage is not None:\n        details.setdefault(\"claude_cli_usage\", usage)\n    envelope[\"details\"] = details\n\n    return envelope\n\n\ndef _rejected_result_recorded(submit_stdout: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"True when the submit step's answer is a kernel-recorded REJECTED result row.\n\n    `aria_kernel agent submit-result` prints its result document on stdout and\n    exits 1 for anything but `accepted`. When that document carries a result\n    row with status `rejected`, the kernel has already appended the row: the\n    claim is terminal (`_assert_lifecycle_mutation_allowed`) and the request\n    derives REJECTED (`derive_request_state`, results dominate). Anything else\n    on a non-zero exit is a refusal before the row and leaves the claim live.\n    \"\"\"\n    text = submit_stdout or \"\"\n    start = text.find(\"{\")\n    if start < 0:\n        return False\n    try:\n        payload = json.loads(text[start:])\n    except ValueError:\n        return False\n    row = payload.get(\"row\") if isinstance(payload, dict) else None\n    return (isinstance(payload, dict) and payload.get(\"status\") == \"rejected\"\n            and isinstance(row, dict) and row.get(\"row_type\") == \"result\" and row.get(\"status\") == \"rejected\")\n\n\ndef _held_request_state(request_id: str, base_dir: Path) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"The lease guard's question to the kernel: what state is the request in?\n\n    None when the kernel has no such request — nothing can be held, so the\n    guard has nothing to hand back (the mocked executor fixtures claim a\n    request that was never minted). `derive_request_state` refuses an\n    unknown id, and a refusal the guard could not tell from an unreadable\n    ledger would make it release on every mocked run.\n    \"\"\"\n    from aria_kernel.agent_invocations import list_agent_invocation_requests\n\n    if not list_agent_invocation_requests(request_id=request_id, base_dir=base_dir):\n        return None\n    return _derive_request_state(request_id=request_id, base_dir=base_dir)\n\n\ndef _rejected_only_for_verification_unavailable(submit_stdout: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Did the kernel reject the submission SOLELY because it could not verify?\n\n    The kernel CLI prints its verdict as JSON on stdout: on a rejection,\n    ``reasons`` (prose, for the ledger and the operator) and\n    ``rejection_codes`` (one machine code per reason). This reads the codes\n    — never the prose — and answers True only when every code says the\n    kernel's evidence probe could not run (git did not answer inside its\n    bound on a loaded host). That is the harness's gap, not the work's: the\n    same envelope resubmitted on a quiet host usually verifies, and it must\n    not burn the request's requeue budget the way `submit_rejected` does\n    (the 2026-08-09 `baseline_unavailable` class, at the acceptance seam).\n\n    Anything else — unparseable output, a kernel exception, no codes, or\n    ONE code that is about the work — is the request's fault: fail toward\n    the human, never toward silent infinite retry.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        payload = json.loads(submit_stdout or \"\")\n    except ValueError:\n        return False\n    if not isinstance(payload, dict) or payload.get(\"status\") != \"rejected\":\n        return False\n    codes = payload.get(\"rejection_codes\")\n    if not isinstance(codes, list) or not codes:\n        return False\n    return all(\n        isinstance(code, str) and code in _EVIDENCE_VERIFICATION_UNAVAILABLE_CODES\n        for code in codes\n    )","numLines":200,"startLine":2584,"totalLines":5532}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CjA6jcaLazAWvCUT4TUqv8","type":"tool_result","content":"70\t    if normalized == \"informational\":\n71\t        return \"low\"\n72\t    return fallback\n73\t\n74\t\n75\tdef _verdict_field(details: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n76\t    \"\"\"The judge .md prompts shape verdicts under details.verdict (evidence,\n77\t    adversarial) or details.consensus (arbiter). Return whichever exists.\n78\t    \"\"\"\n79\t    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), dict):\n80\t        return dict(details[\"verdict\"])\n81\t    if isinstance(details.get(\"consensus\"), dict):\n82\t        return dict(details[\"consensus\"])\n83\t    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), str):\n84\t        # Flat shape (ORPHAN-HIGH-629 sibling): the judge contract file says\n85\t        # BOTH \"Return JSON with: verdict, judge_id, confidence...\" and\n86\t        # \"details.verdict.rationale\". The first live judge run answered\n87\t        # flat, was ACCEPTED by the envelope gate, and dropped HERE with\n88\t        # \"expected verdict ..., got None\" — a verdict lost between two\n89\t        # halves of one document. Honour both spellings; the envelope gate\n90\t        # stays the only authority on validity.\n91\t        return {\n92\t            key: details.get(key)\n93\t            for key in (\n94\t                \"verdict\", \"tool_id\", \"run_id\", \"finding_id\", \"judge_id\",\n95\t                \"model\", \"prompt_hash\", \"confidence\", \"rationale\",\n96\t                \"evidence_refs\", \"judgment_group_id\", \"severity\",\n97\t            )\n98\t            if details.get(key) is not None\n99\t        }\n100\t    return {}\n101\t\n102\t\n103\tdef typed_verdict_block(verdict_block: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n104\t    \"\"\"The verdict block when it is a TYPED answer (typed-judgment plan).\n105\t\n106\t    A typed block names its ``primitive`` (``choice`` for the binary judge\n107\t    roles), carries ``value`` instead of ``verdict``, cites evidence by\n108\t    ``{index, quote}`` and never names a path. A legacy block names\n109\t    ``verdict`` and carries its own ``evidence_refs``; both remain valid, and\n110\t    each is validated by its own law.\n111\t    \"\"\"\n112\t    if verdict_block.get(\"primitive\") in PRIMITIVES:\n113\t        return dict(verdict_block)\n114\t    return None\n115\t\n116\t\n117\tdef _typed_question_for_request(request: dict[str, Any]) -> ChoiceQuestion:\n118\t    \"\"\"The request re-expressed as the choice the typed judge answered.\n119\t\n120\t    Options are the closed verdict vocabulary; refs and pinned excerpts are\n121\t    the request's own, so an index resolves to the request's ref and a quote\n122\t    is checked against the excerpt the mint pinned.\n123\t    \"\"\"\n124\t    refs = [str(ref) for ref in (request.get(\"evidence_refs\") or []) if isinstance(ref, str) and ref.strip()]\n125\t    excerpts = request.get(\"evidence_excerpts\")\n126\t    return ChoiceQuestion(\n127\t        question_id=str(request.get(\"request_id\") or \"request\"),\n128\t        prompt=str(request.get(\"suggested_prompt\") or \"judge the finding\"),\n129\t        options=tuple(FEEDBACK_VERDICTS),"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"66962382-f920-48a2-964d-9f75acea2f64","uuid":"cfe49caa-0d0b-48e8-ab67-542b8758c2b3","timestamp":"2026-09-21T01:35:08.206Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-214613d2c9c1/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/judgment_bridge.py","content":"    if normalized == \"informational\":\n        return \"low\"\n    return fallback\n\n\ndef _verdict_field(details: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"The judge .md prompts shape verdicts under details.verdict (evidence,\n    adversarial) or details.consensus (arbiter). Return whichever exists.\n    \"\"\"\n    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), dict):\n        return dict(details[\"verdict\"])\n    if isinstance(details.get(\"consensus\"), dict):\n        return dict(details[\"consensus\"])\n    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), str):\n        # Flat shape (ORPHAN-HIGH-629 sibling): the judge contract file says\n        # BOTH \"Return JSON with: verdict, judge_id, confidence...\" and\n        # \"details.verdict.rationale\". The first live judge run answered\n        # flat, was ACCEPTED by the envelope gate, and dropped HERE with\n        # \"expected verdict ..., got None\" — a verdict lost between two\n        # halves of one document. Honour both spellings; the envelope gate\n        # stays the only authority on validity.\n        return {\n            key: details.get(key)\n            for key in (\n                \"verdict\", \"tool_id\", \"run_id\", \"finding_id\", \"judge_id\",\n                \"model\", \"prompt_hash\", \"confidence\", \"rationale\",\n                \"evidence_refs\", \"judgment_group_id\", \"severity\",\n            )\n            if details.get(key) is not None\n        }\n    return {}\n\n\ndef typed_verdict_block(verdict_block: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n    \"\"\"The verdict block when it is a TYPED answer (typed-judgment plan).\n\n    A typed block names its ``primitive`` (``choice`` for the binary judge\n    roles), carries ``value`` instead of ``verdict``, cites evidence by\n    ``{index, quote}`` and never names a path. A legacy block names\n    ``verdict`` and carries its own ``evidence_refs``; both remain valid, and\n    each is validated by its own law.\n    \"\"\"\n    if verdict_block.get(\"primitive\") in PRIMITIVES:\n        return dict(verdict_block)\n    return None\n\n\ndef _typed_question_for_request(request: dict[str, Any]) -> ChoiceQuestion:\n    \"\"\"The request re-expressed as the choice the typed judge answered.\n\n    Options are the closed verdict vocabulary; refs and pinned excerpts are\n    the request's own, so an index resolves to the request's ref and a quote\n    is checked against the excerpt the mint pinned.\n    \"\"\"\n    refs = [str(ref) for ref in (request.get(\"evidence_refs\") or []) if isinstance(ref, str) and ref.strip()]\n    excerpts = request.get(\"evidence_excerpts\")\n    return ChoiceQuestion(\n        question_id=str(request.get(\"request_id\") or \"request\"),\n        prompt=str(request.get(\"suggested_prompt\") or \"judge the finding\"),\n        options=tuple(FEEDBACK_VERDICTS),","numLines":60,"startLine":70,"totalLines":499}}}
